_source line 9078 · 2026-05-24T20:59:32.431Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_019Lugi3DRscDamqwrMKMusq`</summary>

```text
19	
20	\begin{abstract}
21	Complete execution traces contain all information needed for verdict prediction, yet they actively degrade supervised fine-tuning (SFT) based learning.
22	Across four code-specialized models spanning three architecture families, compact exception events close an 18--23 percentage-point gap over complete traces, reaching near-perfect accuracy where verbose traces plateau near the source-code-only baseline.
23	A controlled gradient-masking experiment attributes the vast majority of this degradation to content quality at inference rather than training-time gradient dilution: diagnostic tokens are simply too sparse in verbose traces for the model to extract reliably.
24	The practical consequence is striking: with exception-only input a 7B model surpasses GPT-4o by 12 points, a ranking that reverses without trace information, showing that input density dominates model scale.
25	Format-marker removal and cross-dataset transfer confirm the learned signal reflects diagnostic patterns rather than surface shortcuts.
26	These results establish a two-tier signal hierarchy: extract exception events first; when unavailable, tail-truncated traces provide a viable fallback.
27	\end{abstract}
28	
29	\input{introduction}
30	\input{related_work}
```

</details>

```text
1	\section{Conclusion}
2	
3	Across four code-specialized models (1.5B--13B, three families), compact exception events (${\geq}$99.33\%) outperform complete traces (77--82\%) by 18--23pp.
4	The question was never why exceptions help, but why complete traces hurt: gradient masking, cross-dataset transfer, and marker-removal robustness all point to diagnostic density rather than information quantity as the driver of SFT learning.
5	For practitioners, exception extraction should be the default preprocessing; when unavailable, tail-truncated traces (${\geq}$512 tokens) recover 93--98\%.
6	That a 7B SFT model surpasses GPT-4o by 12pp under compact input confirms that input curation outweighs model scaling for this task.
7	
```

34	\input{conclusion}
35	
36	\section*{Limitations}
37	
38	All experiments use Python-only benchmarks (HumanEval, MBPP-sanitized; 591 problems with simple assert-based tests); generalization to other languages, non-assert test frameworks, and multi-file projects is unverified.
39	Python's structured exception hierarchy makes exception events particularly informative; languages with less structured error reporting (e.g., C/C++ segmentation faults) may exhibit different signal distributions.
40	All four models (1.5B--13B) are code-specialized; 30B+ models or general-purpose LLMs may behave differently, and whether larger models can overcome signal dilution in verbose traces is untested.
41	Core results are validated with 3 seeds; supplementary comparisons use single-seed runs with Wilson CI estimates for variance.
42	The gradient masking experiment isolates backward-pass dilution but does not quantify forward-pass attention effects, leaving a residual confound between gradient and inference-time factors.
43	
44	\bibliography{references}
45

when unavailable, tail-truncated traces provide a viable fallback.\n\\end{abstract}\n\n\\input{introduction}\n\\input{related_work}\n\\input{method}\n\\input{experiments}\n\\input{discussion}\n\\input{conclusion}\n\n\\section*{Limitations}\n\nAll experiments use Python-only benchmarks (HumanEval, MBPP-sanitized;     "originalFile": "\\documentclass[11pt,a4paper]{article}\n\\usepackage[review]{acl}\n% \\usepackage{acl}  % uncomment for camera-ready (remove [review])\n\\usepackage{times,latexsym}\n\\usepackage{amsmath,amssymb}\n\\usepackage{graphicx}\n\\graphicspath{{../../figures/paper/}}\n\\usepackage{booktabs}\n\\usepackage{multirow}\n\\usepackage{xcolor}\n\\usepackage{algorithm}\n\\usepackage{algorithmic}\n\n\\title{Complete Execution Traces Degrade Verdict Prediction: \\\\A Diagnostic Signal Analysis}\n\\author{Anonymous ACL submission}\n\n\\begin{document}\n\\maketitle\n\n\\begin{abstract}\nComplete execution traces contain all information needed for verdict prediction, yet they actively degrade supervised fine-tuning (SFT) based learning.\nAcross four code-specialized models spanning three architecture families, compact exception events close an 18--23 percentage-point gap over complete traces, reaching near-perfect accuracy where verbose traces plateau near the source-code-only baseline.\nA controlled gradient-masking experiment attributes the vast majority of this degradation to content quality at inference rather than training-time gradient dilution: diagnostic tokens are simply too sparse in verbose traces for the model to extract reliably.\nThe practical consequence is striking: with exception-only input a 7B model surpasses GPT-4o by 12 points, a ranking that reverses without trace information, showing that input density dominates model scale.\nFormat-marker removal and cross-dataset transfer indicate the learned signal reflects diagnostic patterns rather than surface shortcuts.\nThese results establish a two-tier signal hierarchy: extract exception events first; when unavailable, tail-truncated traces provide a viable fallback.\n\\end{abstract}\n\n\\input{introduction}\n\\input{related_work}\n\\input{method}\n\\input{experiments}\n\\input{discussion}\n\\input{conclusion}\n\n\\section*{Limitations}\n\nAll experiments use Python-only benchmarks (HumanEval, MBPP-sanitized; 591 problems with simple assert-based tests); generalization to other languages, non-assert test frameworks, and multi-file projects is unverified.\nPython's structured exception hierarchy makes exception events particularly informative; languages with less structured error reporting (e.g., C/C++ segmentation faults) may exhibit different signal distributions.\nAll four models (1.5B--13B) are code-specialized; 30B+ models or general-purpose LLMs may behave differently, and whether larger models can overcome signal dilution in verbose traces is untested.\nCore results are validated with 3 seeds; supplementary comparisons use single-seed runs with Wilson CI estimates for variance.\nThe gradient masking experiment isolates backward-pass dilution but does not quantify forward-pass attention effects, leaving a residual confound between gradient and inference-time factors.\n\n\\bibliography{references}\n\n\\appendix\n\\input{appendix}\n\n\\end{document}\n",

    "newString": "VOTA's longer sequences are vulnerable to tokenizer-driven truncation: 1 of 12 model-seed combinations suffered catastrophic failure (DeepSeek seed 42: 84.45\\%), indicating that VOTA robustness depends on tokenizer efficiency.\nThe gradient masking experiment isolates backward-pass dilution but does not quantify forward-pass attention effects, leaving a residual confound between gradient and inference-time factors.",
    "originalFile": "\\documentclass[11pt,a4paper]{article}\n\\usepackage[review]{acl}\n% \\usepackage{acl}  % uncomment for camera-ready (remove [review])\n\\usepackage{times,latexsym}\n\\usepackage{amsmath,amssymb}\n\\usepackage{graphicx}\n\\graphicspath{{../../figures/paper/}}\n\\usepackage{booktabs}\n\\usepackage{multirow}\n\\usepackage{xcolor}\n\\usepackage{algorithm}\n\\usepackage{algorithmic}\n\n\\title{Complete Execution Traces Degrade Verdict Prediction: \\\\A Diagnostic Signal Analysis}\n\\author{Anonymous ACL submission}\n\n\\begin{document}\n\\maketitle\n\n\\begin{abstract}\nComplete execution traces contain all information needed for verdict prediction, yet they actively degrade supervised fine-tuning (SFT) based learning.\nAcross four code-specialized models spanning three architecture families, compact exception events close an 18--23 percentage-point gap over complete traces, reaching near-perfect accuracy where verbose traces plateau near the source-code-only baseline.\nA controlled gradient-masking experiment attributes the vast majority of this degradation to content quality at inference rather than training-time gradient dilution: diagnostic tokens are simply too sparse in verbose traces for the model to extract reliably.\nThe practical consequence is striking: with exception-only input a 7B model surpasses GPT-4o by 12 points, a ranking that reverses without trace information, showing that input density dominates model scale.\nFormat-marker removal and cross-dataset transfer indicate the learned signal reflects diagnostic patterns rather than surface shortcuts.\nThese results establish an empirical two-tier signal hierarchy: extract exception events first; when unavailable, tail-truncated traces provide a viable fallback.\n\\end{abstract}\n\n\\input{introduction}\n\\input{related_work}\n\\input{method}\n\\input{experiments}\n\\input{discussion}\n\\input{conclusion}\n\n\\section*{Limitations}\n\nAll experiments use Python-only benchmarks (HumanEval, MBPP-sanitized; 591 problems with simple assert-based tests); generalization to other languages, non-assert test frameworks, and multi-file projects is unverified.\nPython's structured exception hierarchy makes exception events particularly informative; languages with less structured error reporting (e.g., C/C++ segmentation faults) may exhibit different signal distributions.\nAll four models (1.5B--13B) are code-specialized; 30B+ models or general-purpose LLMs may behave differently, and whether larger models can overcome signal dilution in verbose traces is untested.\nCore results are validated with 3 seeds; supplementary comparisons use single-seed runs with Wilson CI estimates for variance.\nThe gradient masking experiment isolates backward-pass dilution but does not quantify forward-pass attention effects, leaving a residual confound between gradient and inference-time factors.\n\n\\bibliography{references}\n\n\\appendix\n\\input{appendix}\n\n\\end{document}\n",

  "task_prompt": "## 任务：同步论文到远程服务器并编译 PDF\n\n### 目标\n将本地论文 .tex 文件同步到远程服务器 westd-11235，clean compile 生成 PDF，确认 0 errors 且主体 ≤8 页。\n\n### 环境\n- 服务器：westd-11235（root）\n- 远程项目路径：/root/trace-verdict-study\n- 本地论文目录：/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_4/projects/trace-verdict-study/docs/paper/\n- 远程论文目录：/root/trace-verdict-study/docs/paper/\n\n### 步骤\n\n1. **同步 .tex 文件到远程**\n   将以下本地文件同步到远程 /root/trace-verdict-study/docs/paper/：\n   - main.tex\n   - introduction.tex\n   - method.tex\n   - experiments.tex\n   - discussion.tex\n   - related_work.tex\n   - conclusion.tex\n   - appendix.tex\n   - references.bib\n   - arr_checklist.tex（如果存在）\n   \n   使用 scp 或 rsync 将本地文件推送到远程。\n\n2. **远程 clean compile**\n   在远程服务器上执行：\n   ```bash\n   cd /root/trace-verdict-study/docs/paper && latexmk -C && latexmk -pdf main.tex\n   ```\n   \n3. **验证结果**\n   - 检查编译是否成功（0 errors）\n   - 检查总页数\n   - 检查是否有 undefined reference 或 citation warnings\n   \n4. **将编译好的 PDF 拉回本地**\n   将远程的 main.pdf 拉回本地 /home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_4/projects/trace-verdict-study/docs/paper/main.pdf\n\n### 验证标准\n- 编译 0 errors\n- 无 undefined reference / citation warnings\n- PDF 生成成功\n\n### 注意\n- 如果远程缺少 LaTeX 包，用 `apt-get install texlive-full` 或对应的包安装\n- 如果远程 latexmk 不可用，用 `pdflatex main.tex && bibtex main && pdflatex main.tex && pdflatex main.tex` 手动编译\n- 编译失败时报告具体错误信息\n",

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   The user (Director) is coordinating EMNLP 2026 ARR paper revisions (DDL 2026-05-25) through a Paper Agent role. This session handled multiple Director instructions:
   - **Continue Round 16 审稿修复**: Fix remaining "confirm" overclaiming instances, run cross-file consistency check, compile
   - **全文审读 + 写作质量优化**: Comprehensive paper quality audit against ACL/EMNLP standards, produce optimization plan
   - **Execute authorized modifications (7 items)**: H1 contribution simplification, H4 em-dash, H2 number density, M3 footnote trim, M4 frontier crossover compression, M2 discussion de-duplication, M1 section rename
   - **提交前最终修复 (5 items, CURRENT)**: Abstract/Conclusion overclaiming fixes, two-pathway "empirical" qualifier, model scale hedge, VOTA tokenizer fragility in Limitations
   - After all fixes: remote clean compile on westd-11235, verify ≤8 pages, sync PDF back

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - EMNLP 2026 ARR: 8-page main text limit (Conclusion included, Limitations/Ethics excluded), unlimited references/appendix
   - Paper Agent communicates ONLY via `paper_agent_post_to_director` (NOT `main_agent_post_to_director`)
   - Evidence strength calibration: "demonstrates/shows" (strong), "indicates" (medium), "suggests" (weak/confounded)
   - Forward-pass confound: gradient masking only affects backward pass; forward-pass attention is unquantified residual
   - Diagnostic token ratio d: Pearson r=0.949 but N=5, df=3 — descriptive only
   - Two-tier signal hierarchy: Tier 1 (exceptions, ≥98.53%) vs Tier 2 (loop compression, 77-86%)
   - Paper writing norms: no em-dashes, no -ing phrase endings, no Furthermore/Moreover chains, numbers serve narrative

3. Files and Code Sections:

   - **main.tex** (Abstract + Limitations + Ethics)
     - Abstract current version (after all fixes):
     ```latex
     \begin{abstract}
     Complete execution traces contain all information needed for verdict prediction, yet they actively degrade supervised fine-tuning (SFT) based learning.
     Across four code-specialized models spanning three architecture families, compact exception events close an 18--23 percentage-point gap over complete traces, reaching near-perfect accuracy where verbose traces plateau near the source-code-only baseline.
     A controlled gradient-masking experiment attributes the vast majority of this degradation to content quality at inference rather than training-time gradient dilution: diagnostic tokens are simply too sparse in verbose traces for the model to extract reliably.
     The practical consequence is striking: with exception-only input a 7B model surpasses GPT-4o by 12 points, a ranking that reverses without trace information, showing that input density dominates model scale.
     Format-marker removal and cross-dataset transfer indicate the learned signal reflects diagnostic patterns rather than surface shortcuts.
     These results establish an empirical two-tier signal hierarchy: extract exception events first; when unavailable, tail-truncated traces provide a viable fallback.
     \end{abstract}
     ```
     - Limitations current version (after VOTA addition):
     ```latex
     \section*{Limitations}
     
     All experiments use Python-only benchmarks (HumanEval, MBPP-sanitized; 591 problems with simple assert-based tests); generalization to other languages, non-assert test frameworks, and multi-file projects is unverified.
     Python's structured exception hierarchy makes exception events particularly informative; languages with less structured error reporting (e.g., C/C++ segmentation faults) may exhibit different signal distributions.
     All four models (1.5B--13B) are code-specialized; 30B+ models or general-purpose LLMs may behave differently, and whether larger models can overcome signal dilution in verbose traces is untested.
     Core results are validated with 3 seeds; supplementary comparisons use single-seed runs with Wilson CI estimates for variance.
     VOTA's longer sequences are vulnerable to tokenizer-driven truncation: 1 of 12 model-seed combinations suffered catastrophic failure (DeepSeek seed 42: 84.45\%), indicating that VOTA robustness depends on tokenizer efficiency.
     The gradient masking experiment isolates backward-pass dilution but does not quantify forward-pass attention effects, leaving a residual confound between gradient and inference-time factors.
     ```

   - **conclusion.tex** (after final fix):
     ```latex
     \section{Conclusion}
     
     Across four code-specialized models (1.5B--13B, three families), compact exception events (${\geq}$99.33\%) outperform complete traces (77--82\%) by 18--23pp.
     The question was never why exceptions help, but why complete traces hurt: gradient masking, cross-dataset transfer, and marker-removal robustness all point to diagnostic density rather than information quantity as the driver of SFT learning.
     For practitioners, exception extraction should be the default preprocessing; when unavailable, tail-truncated traces (${\geq}$512 tokens) recover 93--98\%.
     That a 7B SFT model surpasses GPT-4o by 12pp under compact input suggests that, within the ${\leq}$13B code-specialized models studied, input curation matters more than model scaling for this task; whether this holds at 30B+ or for general-purpose LLMs remains open.
     ```

   - **introduction.tex** (multiple edits across sessions):
     - Line 15: `---an` → `, an` (em-dash fix)
     - Contributions C1-C4 simplified from ~13 lines to ~8 lines
     - C1 after: `Complete traces (77--82\%) remain near source-code-only levels while compact exception events close an 18--23pp gap to ${\geq}$99.33\%`
     - C2 after: `marker removal collapses a format-exploiting regex by 69pp while SFT drops only 2pp; cross-dataset transfer (99.4--100\%) shows the learned signal generalizes beyond format markers`
     - C3 after: `exception/assertion signals (${\geq}$98.53\%) dominate loop compression (77--86\%) by 12+pp with non-overlapping CIs; a sharp transition at ${\sim}$512 tail tokens yields a practical fallback`
     - C4 after: `controlled gradient masking attributes ${\leq}$4\% of the degradation to gradient dilution, assigning the majority to content quality at inference, though forward-pass attention is an unquantified residual`

   - **experiments.tex** (multiple edits):
     - 4 "confirm" → shows/indicates/illustrates (lines 130, 195, 197, 205)
     - Exception dominance paragraph compressed: inline per-model numbers replaced with `Table~\ref{tab:cross_model}` reference
     - Paragraph renamed: "Exception signals across model scales" → "Scale effects concentrate in Tier~2"
     - Table 1 footnotes: 6 lines → 3 lines (kept only DeepSeek VOTA outlier + CodeLlama proxy + Appendix pointer)
     - Frontier crossover: 5 lines → 2 lines
     - §3.5: "Additional Analyses" → "Complementary Analyses"

   - **discussion.tex** (de-duplication):

   - **appendix.tex** (~16 "confirm" instances softened):

   - **related_work.tex**: 1 "confirm" left unchanged (line 20: describes prior work's empirical findings)

        "content": "\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{Explicit pathway: diagnostic tokens.}\nThe diagnostic token ratio $d$ (\\S2.1) correlates strongly with accuracy, and all four models achieve near-perfect accuracy under \\texttt{exception\\_only} (Table~\\ref{tab:cross_model}); \\texttt{ae\\_only}, a strict superset, slightly underperforms (Table~\\ref{tab:ablation}), confirming that extra content introduces noise even within exception events.\n\n\\paragraph{Implicit pathway: terminal execution state.}\nTail-truncated traces achieve 93--98\\% accuracy (Table~\\ref{tab:tail_cross_model}) despite $d {\\approx} 0$: at ${\\geq}$512 tokens, tail truncation captures terminal variable values implicitly encoding verdict direction; below 384 tokens, the globals dump consumes the budget, yielding near-\\texttt{no\\_trace} performance.\n\n\\paragraph{Signal dilution in complete traces.}\nBoth pathways' signals exist in full traces but are buried under thousands of intermediate tokens.\nControlled gradient masking (\\S\\ref{sec:density}) attributes ${\\leq}$4\\% of the gap to gradient dilution; the majority reflects content quality at inference, as diagnostic tokens are too sparse for reliable extraction (Figure~\\ref{fig:training_dynamics}).\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{training_dynamics}\n\\caption{Training dynamics: \\texttt{exception\\_only} converges to 99.83\\% by step 150; \\texttt{full\\_trace} with label-only loss oscillates (82--89\\%) without convergence.}\n\\label{fig:training_dynamics}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{Why SFT over regex?}\nSFT retains 97.65\\% under marker removal while regex collapses to 28.49\\% (\\S\\ref{sec:nomarker}), and transfers across datasets with negligible loss---confirming diagnostic semantics beyond surface shortcuts.\n\n\\paragraph{Design implications.}\nException events capture 23--27pp of the ${\\sim}$25pp headroom over source code, while complete traces recover only 3--6pp---counter to the assumption that more information helps \\citep{chen2025tracepile,wang2025dcsh}.\nTier~2 tail truncation is model-dependent (Qwen +10.9pp; CodeLlama +0.9pp; Appendix~\\ref{sec:robustness_analysis}).\n",
        "numLines": 27,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 27
      }
    },
    "displayPath": "../../../docs/paper/discussion.tex"

_source line 9143 · 2026-05-24T21:04:02.841Z_

```json
{
  "parentUuid": "6da4249e-8906-4fb6-9586-d1f01d0ae5da",
  "isSidechain": false,
  "attachment": {
    "type": "file",
    "filename": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_4/projects/trace-verdict-study/docs/paper/introduction.tex",
    "content": {
      "type": "text",
      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_4/projects/trace-verdict-study/docs/paper/introduction.tex",
        "content": "\\section{Introduction}\n\nExecution traces (line-by-line records of a program's runtime behavior) have emerged as a promising input modality for code language models \\citep{liu2023codeexecution,chen2025tracepile,ding2024traced,ding2024semcoder,wang2025dcsh,ni2024next}.\nThe intuition is straightforward: more execution information should help the model make better predictions.\nYet for verdict prediction (classifying whether a test passes or fails), this intuition is wrong.\nComplete traces \\emph{actively degrade} accuracy compared to compact exception events, despite containing strictly more information.\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\textwidth]{fig1_trace_overview}\n\\vspace{-8pt}\n\\caption{Overview of the verdict prediction pipeline. Source code and a test input are executed to produce a full raw trace, from which four conditions extract different representations: \\texttt{no\\_trace} (source code only), \\texttt{full\\_trace} (complete line-by-line trace), VOTA (Verdict-Optimized Trace Abstraction: exception events, assertion comparisons, and loop summaries), and \\texttt{exception\\_only} (only exception/error events). Information density decreases from \\texttt{full\\_trace} to \\texttt{exception\\_only}, yet accuracy follows the opposite direction (Table~\\ref{tab:cross_model}). Appendix~\\ref{sec:vota_examples} provides input/output examples.}\n\\label{fig:trace_overview}\n\\end{figure*}\n\n\nPrior work found trace utility to be task-dependent \\citep{wang2025dcsh}, but focused on generation-oriented tasks where output noise confounds the analysis.\nVerdict prediction offers a cleaner testbed: as binary classification, it isolates the learning signal from generation noise, and controlled component ablation directly measures how trace content affects SFT learning.\nThis approach aligns with recent *ACL precedent: CRUXEval \\citep{gu2024cruxeval} uses execution prediction to probe code reasoning, and NExT \\citep{ni2024next} uses trace-augmented chain-of-thought for program repair.\n\nWe present a systematic component ablation of trace representations for verdict prediction.\nUsing 591 problems from HumanEval \\citep{chen2021evaluating} and Mostly Basic Python Problems--sanitized (MBPP-sanitized) \\citep{austin2021program}, we evaluate four models across three architecture families and 1.5B to 13B parameters: Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B/1.5B-Instruct \\citep{qwen2024coder}, CodeLlama-13B-Instruct \\citep{roziere2024code}, and DeepSeek-Coder-6.7B-Instruct \\citep{deepseek2024coder}.\n\nThe central result: complete traces (77--82\\%) barely improve over source code alone, while compact exception events achieve near-perfect accuracy (${\\geq}$99\\%) across all four models, an 18--23 percentage-point (pp) gap (\\S\\ref{sec:cross_model}).\nA simple regex exploiting a format marker matches this ceiling, so the interesting question is not why exceptions help but \\emph{why complete traces hurt}.\nMarker removal collapses the regex to near-chance while SFT retains $>$97\\%; cross-dataset transfer confirms SFT acquires diagnostic patterns beyond format matching (\\S\\ref{sec:nomarker}).\n\nGradient masking attributes ${\\leq}$4\\% of the degradation to gradient dilution; content quality at inference accounts for the majority (\\S\\ref{sec:density}).\nTraining dynamics corroborate this: \\texttt{exception\\_only} converges within 50 steps while \\texttt{full\\_trace} oscillates without convergence (Appendix~\\ref{sec:training_dynamics}).\nA tail-truncation budget sweep reveals a sharp performance transition near 512 tokens, coinciding with the first appearance of structured execution markers (\\S\\ref{sec:trace_length}).\n\nWhile exception events structurally encode verdict direction (exception present typically implies failure), the non-trivial finding is that providing strictly more information (complete traces containing all exception data) degrades performance by 18--23pp.\nOur contributions:\n\\begin{enumerate}\n\\item \\textbf{Complete traces degrade SFT verdict accuracy} despite containing all diagnostic information: across four models (1.5B--13B, three families), complete traces (77--82\\%) remain near source-code-only levels while compact exception events close an 18--23pp gap to ${\\geq}$99.33\\% (\\S\\ref{sec:cross_model}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Semantic vs.\\ format-marker decomposition}: marker removal collapses a format-exploiting regex by 71pp while SFT drops only 2pp; cross-dataset transfer (99.4--100\\%) shows the learned signal generalizes beyond format markers (\\S\\ref{sec:nomarker}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{A two-tier signal hierarchy and its mechanistic explanation}: exception/assertion signals (${\\geq}$98.53\\%) dominate loop compression (77--86\\%) by 12+pp with non-overlapping CIs, and a sharp transition at ${\\sim}$512 tail tokens yields a practical fallback (\\S\\ref{sec:ablation}, \\S\\ref{sec:trace_length}). Controlled gradient masking attributes ${\\leq}$4\\% of the degradation to gradient dilution, assigning the majority to content quality at inference (\\S\\ref{sec:density}).\n\\end{enumerate}\n",
        "numLines": 41,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 41
      }
    },
    "displayPath": "../../../docs/paper/introduction.tex"
  },

```text
36	\section*{Limitations}
37	
38	All experiments use Python-only benchmarks (HumanEval, MBPP-sanitized; 591 problems with simple assert-based tests); generalization to other languages, non-assert test frameworks, and multi-file projects is unverified.
39	Python's structured exception hierarchy makes exception events particularly informative; languages with less structured error reporting (e.g., C/C++ segmentation faults) may exhibit different signal distributions.
40	All four models (1.5B--13B) are code-specialized; 30B+ models or general-purpose LLMs may behave differently, and whether larger models can overcome signal dilution in verbose traces is untested.
41	Core results are validated with 3 seeds; supplementary comparisons use single-seed runs with Wilson CI estimates for variance.

14	\title{Complete Execution Traces Degrade Verdict Prediction: \\A Diagnostic Signal Analysis}
15	\author{Anonymous ACL submission}
16	
17	\begin{document}
18	\maketitle
19	
20	\begin{abstract}
21	Complete execution traces contain all information needed for verdict prediction, yet they actively degrade supervised fine-tuning (SFT) based learning.
22	Across four code-specialized models spanning three architecture families, compact exception events close an 18--23 percentage-point gap over complete traces, reaching near-perfect accuracy where verbose traces plateau near the source-code-only baseline.
23	A controlled gradient-masking experiment attributes the vast majority of this degradation to content quality at inference rather than training-time gradient dilution: diagnostic tokens are simply too sparse in verbose traces for the model to extract reliably.
24	The practical consequence is striking: with exception-only input a 7B model surpasses GPT-4o by 12 points, a ranking that reverses without trace information, showing that input density dominates model scale.
25	Format-marker removal and cross-dataset transfer confirm the learned signal reflects diagnostic patterns rather than surface shortcuts.
26	These results establish a two-tier signal hierarchy: extract exception events first; when unavailable, tail-truncated traces provide a viable fallback.
27	\end{abstract}
28	
29	\input{introduction}
30	\input{related_work}
31	\input{method}
32	\input{experiments}
33	\input{discussion}
34	\input{conclusion}
35	
36	\section*{Limitations}
37	
38	All experiments use Python-only benchmarks (HumanEval, MBPP-sanitized; 591 problems with simple assert-based tests); generalization to other languages, non-assert test frameworks, and multi-file projects is unverified.
39	Python's structured exception hierarchy makes exception events particularly informative; languages with less structured error reporting (e.g., C/C++ segmentation faults) may exhibit different signal distributions.
40	All four models (1.5B--13B) are code-specialized; 30B+ models or general-purpose LLMs may behave differently, and whether larger models can overcome signal dilution in verbose traces is untested.
41	Core results are validated with 3 seeds; supplementary comparisons use single-seed runs with Wilson CI estimates for variance.
42	The gradient masking experiment isolates backward-pass dilution but does not quantify forward-pass attention effects, leaving a residual confound between gradient and inference-time factors.
43	
44	\bibliography{references}
45	
46	\appendix
47	\input{appendix}
48	
49	\end{document}

```text
1	\section{Conclusion}
2	
3	Across four code-specialized models (1.5B--13B, three families), compact exception events (${\geq}$99.33\%) outperform complete traces (77--82\%) by 18--23pp.
4	The question was never why exceptions help, but why complete traces hurt: gradient masking, cross-dataset transfer, and marker-removal robustness all point to diagnostic density rather than information quantity as the driver of SFT learning.
5	For practitioners, exception extraction should be the default preprocessing; when unavailable, tail-truncated traces (${\geq}$512 tokens) recover 93--98\%.
6	That a 7B SFT model surpasses GPT-4o by 12pp under compact input confirms that input curation outweighs model scaling for this task.
7	
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_018otxthCMheATG9njDpgJnB`</summary>

```text
20	\begin{abstract}
21	Complete execution traces contain all information needed for verdict prediction, yet they actively degrade supervised fine-tuning (SFT) based learning.
22	Across four code-specialized models spanning three architecture families, compact exception events close an 18--23 percentage-point gap over complete traces, reaching near-perfect accuracy where verbose traces plateau near the source-code-only baseline.
23	A controlled gradient-masking experiment attributes the vast majority of this degradation to content quality at inference rather than training-time gradient dilution: diagnostic tokens are simply too sparse in verbose traces for the model to extract reliably.
24	The practical consequence is striking: with exception-only input a 7B model surpasses GPT-4o by 12 points, a ranking that reverses without trace information, showing that input density dominates model scale.
25	Format-marker removal and cross-dataset transfer indicate the learned signal reflects diagnostic patterns rather than surface shortcuts.
26	These results establish an empirical two-tier signal hierarchy: extract exception events first; when unavailable, tail-truncated traces provide a viable fallback.
27	\end{abstract}
28	
29	\input{introduction}
30	\input{related_work}
31	\input{method}
32	\input{experiments}
33	\input{discussion}
34	\input{conclusion}
35	
36	\section*{Limitations}
37	
38	\paragraph{Scope.}
39	All experiments use Python-only benchmarks (HumanEval, MBPP-sanitized; 591 problems with simple assert-based tests); generalization to other languages, non-assert test frameworks, and multi-file projects is unverified.
40	Python's structured exception hierarchy makes exception events particularly informative; languages with less structured error reporting (e.g., C/C++ segmentation faults) may exhibit different signal distributions.
41	All four models (1.5B--13B) are code-specialized; 30B+ models or general-purpose LLMs may behave differently, and whether larger models can overcome signal dilution in verbose traces is untested.
42	
43	\paragraph{Variance and robustness.}
44	Core results are validated with 3 seeds; supplementary comparisons use single-seed runs with Wilson CI estimates for variance.
45	VOTA's longer sequences are vulnerable to tokenizer-driven truncation: 1 of 12 model-seed combinations suffered catastrophic failure (DeepSeek seed 42: 84.45\%), indicating that VOTA robustness depends on tokenizer efficiency.
46	
47	\paragraph{Mechanistic analysis.}
48	The gradient masking experiment isolates backward-pass dilution but does not quantify forward-pass attention effects, leaving a residual confound between gradient and inference-time factors.
49	
50	\bibliography{references}
51	
52	\appendix
53	\input{appendix}
54	
55	\end{document}
56	
```

  "task_type": "paper_compile:limitations_reformat",

  "task_prompt": "## 任务：远程 clean compile 论文 PDF\n\n本地论文 .tex 文件已修改（main.tex, conclusion.tex）。需要同步到远程服务器 westd-11235 并 clean compile，确认 0 errors 且主正文 ≤8 页。\n\n### 步骤\n\n1. **SCP 本地修改的 .tex 文件到远程**：\n   ```bash\n   scp /home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_4/projects/trace-verdict-study/docs/paper/main.tex westd-11235:/root/trace-verdict-study/docs/paper/main.tex\n   scp /home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_4/projects/trace-verdict-study/docs/paper/conclusion.tex westd-11235:/root/trace-verdict-study/docs/paper/conclusion.tex\n   ```\n\n2. **远程 clean compile**：\n   ```bash\n   ssh westd-11235 \"cd /root/trace-verdict-study/docs/paper && latexmk -C && latexmk -pdf main.tex\"\n   ```\n\n3. **验证结果**：\n   - 检查编译输出，确认 0 errors\n   - 检查页数：`ssh westd-11235 \"cd /root/trace-verdict-study/docs/paper && pdfinfo main.pdf | grep Pages\"` 或用 `pdftotext main.pdf - | grep -c $'\\f'` 估算\n   - 确认 Conclusion 在第 8 页内（主正文 ≤8 页），Limitations 不计入页数限制但应在 References 之前\n\n4. **拉回 PDF 到本地**：\n   ```bash\n   scp westd-11235:/root/trace-verdict-study/docs/paper/main.pdf /home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_4/projects/trace-verdict-study/docs/paper/main.pdf\n   ```\n\n5. **报告**：输出编译是否成功、error/warning 数量、总页数。\n",

}

1	\documentclass[11pt,a4paper]{article}
2	\usepackage[review]{acl}
3	% \usepackage{acl}  % uncomment for camera-ready (remove [review])
4	\usepackage{times,latexsym}
5	\usepackage{amsmath,amssymb}
6	\usepackage{graphicx}
7	\graphicspath{{../../figures/paper/}}
8	\usepackage{booktabs}
9	\usepackage{multirow}
10	\usepackage{xcolor}
11	\usepackage{algorithm}
12	\usepackage{algorithmic}
13	
14	\title{Complete Execution Traces Degrade Verdict Prediction: \\A Diagnostic Signal Analysis}
15	\author{Anonymous ACL submission}
16	
17	\begin{document}
18	\maketitle
19	
20	\begin{abstract}
21	Complete execution traces contain all information needed for verdict prediction, yet they actively degrade supervised fine-tuning (SFT) based learning.
22	Across four code-specialized models spanning three architecture families, compact exception events close an 18--23 percentage-point gap over complete traces, reaching near-perfect accuracy where verbose traces plateau near the source-code-only baseline.
23	A controlled gradient-masking experiment attributes the vast majority of this degradation to content quality at inference rather than training-time gradient dilution: diagnostic tokens are simply too sparse in verbose traces for the model to extract reliably.
24	The practical consequence is striking: with exception-only input a 7B model surpasses GPT-4o by 12 points, a ranking that reverses without trace information, showing that input density dominates model scale.
25	Format-marker removal and cross-dataset transfer indicate the learned signal reflects diagnostic patterns rather than surface shortcuts.
26	These results establish an empirical two-tier signal hierarchy: extract exception events first; when unavailable, tail-truncated traces provide a viable fallback.
27	\end{abstract}
28	
29	\input{introduction}
30	\input{related_work}
31	\input{method}
32	\input{experiments}
33	\input{discussion}
34	\input{conclusion}
35	
36	\section*{Limitations}
37	
38	\paragraph{Scope.}
39	All experiments use Python-only benchmarks (HumanEval, MBPP-sanitized; 591 problems with simple assert-based tests); generalization to other languages, non-assert test frameworks, and multi-file projects is unverified.
40	Python's structured exception hierarchy makes exception events particularly informative; languages with less structured error reporting (e.g., C/C++ segmentation faults) may exhibit different signal distributions.
41	All four models (1.5B--13B) are code-specialized; 30B+ models or general-purpose LLMs may behave differently, and whether larger models can overcome signal dilution in verbose traces is untested.
42	
43	\paragraph{Variance and robustness.}
44	Core results are validated with 3 seeds; supplementary comparisons use single-seed runs with Wilson CI estimates for variance.
45	VOTA's longer sequences are vulnerable to tokenizer-driven truncation: 1 of 12 model-seed combinations suffered catastrophic failure (DeepSeek seed 42: 84.45\%), indicating that VOTA robustness depends on tokenizer efficiency.
46	
47	\paragraph{Mechanistic analysis.}
48	The gradient masking experiment isolates backward-pass dilution but does not quantify forward-pass attention effects, leaving a residual confound between gradient and inference-time factors.
49	
50	\bibliography{references}
51	
52	\appendix
53	\input{appendix}
54	
55	\end{document}
56	
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_014wMNJS5FcByWCKnP1mBrhQ`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Conclusion}
2	
3	Across four code-specialized models (1.5B--13B, three families), compact exception events (${\geq}$99.33\%) outperform complete traces (77--82\%) by 18--23pp.
4	The question was never why exceptions help, but why complete traces hurt: gradient masking, cross-dataset transfer, and marker-removal robustness all point to diagnostic density rather than information quantity as the driver of SFT learning.
5	For practitioners, exception extraction should be the default preprocessing; when unavailable, tail-truncated traces (${\geq}$512 tokens) recover 93--98\%.
6	That a 7B SFT model surpasses GPT-4o by 12pp under compact input suggests that, within the ${\leq}$13B code-specialized models studied, input curation matters more than model scaling for this task; whether this holds at 30B+ or for general-purpose LLMs remains open.
7	
```

</details>
